To: Lara Stinson <l.stinson@digitalletterbox.com>
From: Madeline Dupree <madeline@home-realty.net>
Date: August 10
Subject: 1582 Harris Lane

Dear Ms. Stinson,

Great news! I've just received confirmation in writing that the offer you made on the property at 1582 Harris Lane has been accepted. Lacey Kent agreed that it was a fair price. I've attached a list of our business partners, who offer special deals to our customers.

What you need to do next is contact the bank where you had your home loan preapproved, as the home's value will need to be assessed independently by one of the bank's representatives before funds can be released. In addition, we will send Dion Tucker, a home inspector from our office, to check the property. After assessing the property, he will send you a report with information about any potential problems and the approximate costs of fixing them. He will e-mail that report to you on August 18.

You will visit the property on August 22 for a final walk-through. This will give you the opportunity to make sure items that were part of the sale, such as light fixtures or appliances, have not been removed from the property. You can also check for any unexpected damage. If everything is satisfactory, you will meet at my office the following day to sign the documents for the sale, taking legal possession of the property at that time.

If you need help with moving, I highly recommend SMC, whose contact information is listed on the attachment. Please let me know if I can be of assistance with anything else.

All the best.
Madeline Dupree
Associate Realtor, Home Realty

### Job Opening of the Week

Shalimar-Marino is currently seeking a senior architect for its office in Boston. This is a full-time position with a competitive salary and generous annual bonuses. The senior architect is in charge of supervising a team of five junior architects. Responsibilities include setting objectives for projects, assessing the needs of the client, and managing material usage within a budget. As the role is to be carried out with little support from upper management, there is a need to exercise good judgment when working independently. Applicants must have five years of experience and currently hold a valid license. Membership in the National Architectural Association (NAA) is preferred but not required.

Resumes should be sent to Kyle Whitten at kwhitten@shalimarmarino.com by March 31. The company will hold group interviews from April 7 to 11. Those who pass the group interview phase will be invited for an individual interview during the week of April 21.

You have received this message because you opted into job notifications at the time of purchasing your _Architect World Magazine_ subscription. To stop receiving these messages, go to the Communications Preferences page of your online account.

You should e-mail me a portfolio including images of your past projects by this Friday. Should you be offered the position, you will have to provide evidence that you have a valid state license.

I look forward to hearing from you!
Kyle Whitten

To: Casey Irvine <casey@bluewavespa.co.uk>
From: Lillian Blais <lillian@bluewavespa.co.uk>
Date: January 10
Subject: Please read

Dear Casey,

I wanted to fill you in on what you missed at our spa's annual planning meeting. We mainly discussed offering more treatments and how to find out which ones would be best. In preparation for the meeting, I had looked into hiring a professional research company. Sarah Kelm, whom I used to work with at Calvin Hotel, had suggested using Starks Research. However, when I started researching their fees, I quickly realized that it would be unnecessary to hire an outside firm when we can perform the research ourselves.

Therefore, we've decided to hold an event called "Exploratory Week" from February 2 to 8. For this week only, we will bring in specialists to provide some unique treatments to our customers. We will send exclusive event invitations to customers who visit regularly. They will be able to try these new treatments at no charge in exchange for providing their feedback. We can have a maximum of forty people participate in the event, unless Ms. Gould releases some urgent funds.

Lilian

---

### Summary Report of Exploratory Week Event at Blue Wave Spa

**Owner:** Maya Gould
**Research prepared by:** Raymond Biscoe

| Treatment                | Average Customer Rating (1=poor, 5=excellent) | Recommended Price |
| :----------------------- | :-------------------------------------------- | :---------------- |
| Cell Rejuvenation Facial | 4.2                                           | £160              |
| Crystal Body Massage     | 4.8                                           | £140              |
| Oxygen Boost             | 3.9                                           | £95               |
| Whipped Cocoa Bath       | 3.7                                           | £125              |

A total of 63 clients—44 women and 19 men—participated in the event. Each participant received 1–3 treatments, depending on his or her schedule and preferences. It has been agreed that the spa will introduce the most popular treatment first, followed by the most expensive one.

**Sunday, August 9, 9 A.M.-4 P.M. / Flower Spectacular**

**Questions 191-195 refer to the following flyer and e-mails.**

### Water for the World Festival

**Saturday, June 16, at Sunburst Hall, Seattle**

An estimated 780 million people around the world do not have clean drinking water, and 80% of illnesses in developing countries are related to poor-quality drinking water.\*

There is something you can do to help!

Be a part of our fundraising efforts on Saturday, June 16, from 10 A.M. to 8 P.M. at Sunburst Hall. The event will include activities such as a coloring contest for children, a water-carrying race for teens, and informative lectures for adults. A suggested donation of $10 will be collected at the entrance. The hall has been generously donated to the cause. Therefore, 100% of the collected funds go directly to well-building efforts in Afghanistan, Cambodia, and Haiti—nations with a pressing need for clean water.

The event is sponsored by the Drop of Care charity. Its founder, Aubrey Robertson (author of best-seller _Thirst for Life_), will give a talk at the festival as well as at the Leesburg Center on June 18.

\*Find more facts like these on dropofcare.org.

---

To: Aubrey Robertson <aubreyrobertson@dropofcare.org>
From: Brian Mitchell <brian@weunite.org>
Date: June 21
Subject: We Unite

Dear Mr. Robertson,

I am the director of a charity called "We Unite," which provides funding to causes related to global health. I was in Seattle on a business trip earlier this week, and I attended your talk at the Leesburg Center. I found your insights to be fascinating, and more than ever I was aware that I should count myself lucky for having access to clean drinking water. I would like to explore how our two organizations could work together. Would you be able to meet me at our head office sometime in July? I've attached information about how to get to our site.

Warmest regards,
Brian Mitchell

---

To: Brian Mitchell <brian@weunite.org>
From: Aubrey Robertson <aubreyrobertson@dropofcare.org>
Date: June 24
Subject: RE: We Unite

Dear Mr. Mitchell,

I was surprised and pleased to hear from you. I recently read your book on the economics of water, and I've recommended it to several colleagues. I would really like to meet you in person, as you suggested, to discuss a possible partnership. Fortunately, I will be in Vancouver from July 14 to 17. Please call me at 206-555-0199 to set up the details.

All the best,
Aubrey Robertson

### NOTICE TO SHIMMER JEWELRY CUSTOMERS

After nearly two decades of service to the Huntsville community at its Powley Street location, Shimmer Jewelry will be going out of business, with April 30 as our final business day. It has been a pleasure serving you over the years, and we are honored that we have been a part of so many weddings, anniversaries, birthdays, and other celebrations. Although our jewelry repair services will no longer be available, you'll still be able to purchase some of the merchandise styles from our jewelry designers. Susan Marlow plans to start her own jewelry Web site, and Amy Sabo will open a small shop on Custer Avenue. We hope you will support their endeavors.

Thank you for your patronage.
Christine Chenoweth
Owner, Shimmer Jewelry

### Article

May 25 - Grand City's downtown shopping district, once a bustling area of activity, is suffering due to skyrocketing rents. Rental fees in the area have increased by nearly 30% over the past few years, driving small businesses out. For example, local business owner Christine Chenoweth said her store went out of business because of these conditions. Another shop owner, Gloria Lohman, said she relocated to the Clifton Mall, where rents were nearly half the price. City Manager Kyle Trevino reported that the 2% increase in sales tax, passed by voters last fall, is also putting pressure on businesses.

The local business community is seeking solutions to keep the economy moving in Grand City. The number of festivals, live performances, and other events that draw crowds has declined dramatically. "Rents and taxes are just part of the equation," the city manager said. "If we plan more activities in the area, we can bring in more business."
